I just feel low and worn down, but not like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My staff has pointed out that in meetings I take longer to answer questions, like there’s this pause before I can get my thoughts and words out.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had thoughts about wanting to die or wishing I wouldn’t wake up. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things at home, but I’m slower with decisions, and I’ve been less reliable about family plans. I’ve also pulled back from my art activities, which usually help me stay connected.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of being down and exhausted are affecting your ability to work or take care of things at home significantly?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I’m still getting to work and handling things at home, but it’s taking more effort. I’m slower making decisions, I’ve been less dependable with family plans, and I’ve pulled back from my art stuff, which usually helps me stay connected.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current low mood as being consistently severe or are there variations in how down you feel from day to day?\n\nPatient: It’s not severe all the time, no. It’s almost like I’m disappointed in the day before it even starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I usually really enjoy painting, and I like just wandering around museum exhibits, but lately I don’t even touch my supplies. And when I think about going to a museum, I just… can’t make myself care enough to go.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. Painting was the main thing I still used to enjoy, and going through museum exhibits, but lately I just leave the supplies sitting there. Even the idea of going to a museum doesn’t really appeal to me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over things in my head, like family plans I missed or conversations with my husband that got tense. I end up feeling like I’m letting everyone down — as a wife, a mother, and even at work as a manager.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level lately, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yes, a little. I don’t feel restless exactly, but I’ve noticed I’m slower. My staff has pointed out that in meetings I take longer to answer questions, like there’s this pause before I can get my thoughts and words out.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had thoughts about wanting to die or wishing I wouldn’t wake up. I just feel low and worn down, but not like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About a month, I’d say. It started with me feeling unusually energized and driven, and then it shifted into this low feeling that’s been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little. I’m not trying to lose weight, but I’ve been picking at meals or just forgetting to eat lunch when I’m at work. Some of my skirts are fitting a bit looser than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or seen things that other people don’t see. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, especially earlier in the month. I was in this really charged, upbeat mood, like I felt brilliant and full of ideas, but if my husband questioned what I was planning, I’d get irritated really quickly.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual or have trouble getting started with activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, a bit. My staff has actually noticed it more than I have. In meetings, I’m slower to answer questions—like there’s this pause before I can get my thoughts together and the words out.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down and exhausted are interfering with your daily life a lot, or just somewhat?\n\nPatient: I’d say somewhat. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things at home, but I’m slower with decisions, and I’ve been less reliable about family plans. I’ve also pulled back from my art activities, which usually help me stay connected.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of being down and exhausted are affecting your ability to work or take care of things at home significantly?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I’m still getting to work and handling things at home, but it’s taking more effort. I’m slower making decisions, I’ve been less dependable with family plans, and I’ve pulled back from my art stuff, which usually helps me stay connected.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current low mood as being consistently severe or are there variations in how down you feel from day to day?\n\nPatient: It’s not severe all the time, no. It varies some day to day, but most days lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you previously enjoyed but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes. I usually love painting, and I like wandering through museum exhibits, but lately I just… don’t feel drawn to them. My supplies are sitting there untouched, and I can’t really make myself care enough to go to a museum right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life, like tasting favorite foods or enjoying a warm bath, that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Some small things are still okay, I guess, but the bigger things I normally enjoy aren’t really doing much for me. Like painting or going to museum exhibits—I usually love that, but lately I just leave my supplies sitting there and I can’t make myself care enough to go.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you previously did not enjoy but now find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, I can’t think of anything like that. I haven’t really found new things enjoyable lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
